# Canonical judicial cases

One row per unique case in the official SJC/CJB roster. The canonical `case_id` is a zero-padded docket such as `2023-07`; legacy, era-based, and Minutes identifiers are preserved as aliases. See [the taxonomy specification](../docs/JUDICIAL-CASE-TAXONOMY.md) for controlled vocabularies and source rules.

**501 records** · classified 489 · needs review 12 · roster only 0

| `1975-01` | Rev. Harold L. Webb & Rev. Thomas Miller v. Pacific Presbytery | Complaint | Dismissed | Not reached | legacy `1975-1`; Minutes `3-12` | Ministers complained that Pacific Presbytery conducted nonroutine business without the constitutionally required quorum of three teaching and three ruling elders. The Assembly agreed that the August 4, 1975 actions were irregular, but dismissed the complaint because Presbytery later met with a quorum and ratified those actions. The dismissal therefore reflected correction of the defect, not approval of proceeding without a quorum. | `BCO 14-4` | `quorum requirement`, `presbytery competence`, `constitutional violation`, `judicial complaint`, `ratification of actions` | Classified | [case page](../cases/ga03_1975__case1.md) |
| `1976-01` | Session of Eastland Presbyterian Church, Memphis, Tennessee v. Covenant Presbytery | Complaint | Not sustained | Not stated | legacy `1976-1`; Minutes `4-13` | A session complained that Covenant Presbytery violated PCA standards by approving the summer employment of a divorced and remarried man in a ministerial capacity. It expressly did not decide whether such employment would violate the Constitution. The General Assembly adopted the report as its action and judgment, leaving the presbytery's action undisturbed. | — | `ministerial employment`, `jurisdiction`, `complaint validity` | Classified | [case page](../cases/ga04_1976__case2.md) |

| `1977-01` | Rev. Paul L. Karlberg and Ruling Elder James G. Luce v. | `2007-14` | PCA v. Louisiana Presbytery (Trial) | BCO 40-5 matter | Guilty; Dismissed | BCO 40-5 — important delinquency or grossly unconstitutional proceeding | — | In this BCO 40-5 proceeding over Louisiana Presbytery’s investigation of TE Stephen Wilkins, the SJC dismissed Specification 1 because it could not find that the cited procedures were constitutionally required or that guilt was proved. On Presbytery’s guilty plea to that specification, the SJC imposed a public admonition for failing to find a strong presumption of doctrinal guilt and pursue trial. | `2016-16` | Sartorius v. Siouxlands Presbytery | Complaint | Denied | Not stated | — | Did a presbytery fail in its duty to condemn erroneous opinions by acquitting a minister on theological-error charges? The SJC denied the complaint, leaving the acquittal undisturbed. A complaint against a not-guilty verdict cannot provoke a retrial at the superior court; under BCO 43 the superior court may only annul or remand, not reverse, and the complainant bore the burden to show error from the record. The record contained the defendant's testimony, including recantations or clarifications, Defense Exhibit 1, and witness testimony sufficient to refrain from contradicting the trial court. The SJC expressly did not decide the defendant's actual views. A separate concurrence elaborated deference standards but did not change the judgment. | `BCO 13-9.f`, `BCO 15-3`, `BCO 31-2`, `BCO 35-1`, `BCO 35-5`, `BCO 39-3`, `BCO 39-3.1`, `BCO 39-3.2`, `BCO 39-3.3`, `BCO 39-3.4`, `BCO 43-2`, `BCO 43-5`, `BCO 43-7`, `BCO 43-9`, `BCO 43-10`, `BCO 42-6` | `Federal Vision theology`, `New Perspective on Paul`, `covenant of works`, `baptism and saving benefits`, `perseverance of the saints`, `assurance`, `acquittal complaint`, `standards of review`, `BCO 39-3 deference`, `clear error`, `factual finding`, `double jeopardy`, `condemning erroneous opinions`, `BCO 13-9.f`, `retrial`, `Westminster Standards`, `heresy charges`, `judicial commission procedure` | Classified | [case page](../cases/ga46_2018__2016-16.md) |

| `2023-09` | Myers v. Illiana Presbytery | Appeal | Sustained; Reversed | Not stated | legacy `2023-9` | An elder appealed Presbytery’s elevation, without additional process, of suspensions from office and the Sacraments to deposition and excommunication. The SJC granted the appeal, reversed in whole, and remanded, restoring the prior suspensions. Elevation required a hearing on incorrigibility and contumacy—with a charge, evidence, plea, and defense—but not a full de novo trial; disagreement with the original verdict alone was insufficient. The SJC left unresolved deposition from office when excommunication is absent. | `BCO 11-2`, `BCO 11-3`, `BCO 11-4`, `BCO 13-2`, `BCO 30-1`, `BCO 30-3`, `BCO 30-4`, `BCO 31-2`, `BCO 32-2`, `BCO 32-3`, `BCO 32-4`, `BCO 32-5`, `BCO 32-6`, `BCO 32-7`, `BCO 32-13`, `BCO 32-15`, `BCO 32-18`, `BCO 33-2`, `BCO 34-10`, `BCO 35-1`, `BCO 35-2`, `BCO 35-4`, `BCO 35-9`, `BCO 36-6`, `BCO 37-2`, `BCO 37-3`, `BCO 37-4`, `BCO 37-7`, `BCO 37-8`, `BCO 38-2`, `BCO 39-3`, `BCO 42-3`, `BCO 42-6`, `BCO 46-8`, `BCO 35-15`, `BCO 27-4`, `BCO 26-2`, `BCO 32-8` | `elevation of censure`, `excommunication`, `deposition from office`, `indefinite suspension from sacraments`, `due process in church discipline`, `contumacy`, `incorrigibility`, `judicial process vs. parliamentary process`, `de novo trial`, `censure without additional process` | Classified | [case page](../cases/ga51_2024__2023-09.md) |

It remanded for Presbytery to adjudicate the filing as a complaint at its next stated meeting; denial or failure to act permits review by the SJC. | `2025-13` | Felipe Assis and Carter Brown v. South Florida Presbytery | Appeal | Sustained; Reversed | Great deference unless clear error (discretion and judgment); Independent review (constitutional interpretation) | — | Two ministers appealed Presbytery’s decision to divest them of office without censure after their congregations left the PCA. The SJC sustained the BCO-violation specification and reversed the divestitures: their calls had never been formally dissolved, and BCO 34-10 did not apply because they continued performing ministerial functions and remained accepted by their churches. It declined as unable to judge claims that Presbytery disregarded their emails or used the procedure as discriminatory pretext.
